- Run ID: se-fullspread-preview-consumers-20261103
- Member: astra
- Profile: research
- Phase: initial independent response
- Requested role: council-member
- Requested model and reasoning: chatgpt-subscription/gpt-6-astra:high
- Actual runtime role: not exposed
- Actual runtime model/provider/account: not exposed
- Actual runtime reasoning: not exposed
- Actual runtime task ID and session ID: not exposed
- Context: fresh; no earlier conversation continued
- Peers read before submission: no
- Owned response: `docs/research/se-fullspread-preview-consumers-20261103/members/astra/response.md`
- Observation date: 2026-11-03

## Question and targets

What is the complete in-repo list of consumers of the old 32-byte `previewExchangeIn(address,uint256,address)` return, selector `0x89d61912`?

The inventory below separates that question from seven-argument `exchangeIn(address,uint256,address,uint256,address,bool,uint256)`, selector `0xd02d3063`. It includes source-level scalar reads, explicit interface declarations, indirect low-level decoders, wrappers, tests, frontend bindings, scripts, generated TypeChain client bindings, and retained historical Solidity sources. It does not reopen F13 or F14.

## Evidence

- **Source observation:** `lib/crane/contracts/interfaces/IStandardExchangeIn.sol:45-48` declares one preview output, `uint256 amountOut`; its execution declaration at `:60-68` also returns one word. Selectors in this report are the brief-supplied identities; no selector-generation command was run.
- **Source observation:** the required blocker reference is `contracts/vaults/standard/exchange/protocols/uniswap/v4/fullSpread/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_BLOCKERS.md:196`. Its deployed-provider route is corroborated by `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/rateProviders/standardExchange/IStandardExchangeRateProviderDFPkg.sol:18-28` and `StandardExchangeRateProviderDFPkg.sol:57-65,74-77,94-100,119-125` in the same directory. The actual consumers are `StandardExchangeRateProviderFacet.sol:102-106,119-125`, not the package deployment itself.
- **Source observation:** exact-length preview gates also exist in the wrapped provider and three Balancer reconciliation probes, individually cited in the first table. Both main-provider branches are included: initial mint when supply is zero, and the live full-book quote.
- **Source observation:** the handwritten frontend preview ABIs are `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/lib/insightsAbi.ts:69-76` and `frontend/packages/protocol/src/protocolDetfAbi.ts:33-40`. The concrete UI consumers and exchange simulations are separately enumerated.
- **Static analysis:** searches were paginated through their final pages. Search results with per-file caps were recovered by reading the remaining file content; this recovered additional Aerodrome/Uniswap invariant preview calls and staking/adversarial exchange calls. Matched regions were read to distinguish consumption from production, selector metadata, comments, failure-only probes, and calls with a different argument count.
- **Source observation:** generated clients are physically present under `typechain/`, although ignored by `.gitignore:10-12`; `hardhat.config.ts:81-83` identifies the artifact/client output locations. All 294 located preview/exchange method or factory-output declarations in that tree were read and have one scalar output. Their individual paths are included, including stale bindings for retired producers.
- Public searches and external documentation: not used. No private source was sent to a search service.

## Assumptions

- A consumer means a source-level use of the returned amount or a binding/decoder that describes that amount. Merely importing the shared interface is not counted as another declaration. Inherited client surfaces are represented by their explicit generated bindings where present.
- A standalone call used only to observe success or revert does not read the amount at source level. Those preview calls are explicitly recorded in the unaffected table; this is not a claim that a compiler can never perform ABI validation for a discarded typed result.
- A producer entry point is not counted merely because its own return type is old. A producer file that also calls another preview appears for its consumer locations. Internal helper functions named exchangeIn are not treated as selector `0xd02d3063` decoders.
- `migrate` is an **inference about ABI adaptation if the consumer is used with the accepted tuple-return surface**, not authority to change every unrelated deployed family. `unaffected` for retained audit sources or retired generated clients means preserve the historical artifact, not that its one-word binding understands the tuple. A future adapted reproduction would need a new/updated working copy, not an alteration of preserved evidence.

## Coverage

| Target | Status | Search and result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Production and colocated test support, `contracts/` | examined | Exact-word `previewExchangeIn` / `exchangeIn`, both hexadecimal selectors, explicit declarations, and scalar low-level decoders. Included hooks, routers, rate providers, DETFs/SY, package validation, and shared test bases. |
| Test suites, `test/` | examined | Same patterns, including fork, debug/archive, invariant, adversarial, lifecycle, native-SY parity, proxy-surface amount reads, and locked/atomic callers. Selector-only checks were separated. |
| Scripts, `scripts/` | examined | Preview hits in RichnessLib and the two research fixtures are listed. Research execution return reads are listed separately. Archived six-argument exchangeIn calls are explicitly excluded from selector `0xd02d3063`. |
| Frontend, `frontend/` | examined | Function-name strings, raw selectors, ABIs, read/simulate calls, and e2e bindings. Four source files contain preview names: two consumers and two ABI definitions. No additional preview consumer was found in the other frontend source files. |
| Vendored tree, `lib/` | examined | Searches across 14,453 Solidity files found the shared interface and ComputeNatSpecValues selector assertions; no other preview/exchange named consumer. Non-Solidity code search for the names/selectors found no hit across 4,171 files. |
| Generated clients, `typechain/` | examined | Ignored files explicitly included. Typed methods and matching factory ABI outputs are both listed, not silently dropped as generated code. |
| Historical source and audit reproductions, `docs/` | examined | Exact names/selectors across Solidity and other executable-source extensions, excluding all members directories. Scalar-consuming retained sources, explicit historical bindings, and generic decoders are included with historical disposition. Reports/JSON evidence strings are not executable consumers. |
| Other repository source/configuration trees and root files | examined | Whole-root code/binding-extension sweep outside the already searched trees, build outputs, dependencies, and session metadata found no further named preview/exchange consumers. This included research, deployment/configuration, and implementation-artifact paths where searchable source files existed. |
| Required rate-provider route | examined | B-08 deployment wiring traced to both main-provider decoder branches; wrapped-provider sibling also examined and listed. |
| Unaffected families | examined | Exact-out previews/execution, multi-token previews, hook-only previews, salt previews, transition quotes, and producer/metadata-only material are explicitly recorded below with their search patterns and source lines. |

Search patterns used: `\bpreviewExchangeIn\b`, `\bexchangeIn\b`, case-insensitive `89d61912` / `d02d3063`, function-name ABI strings, `abi.decode` with `uint256`, and first-word `mload` patterns. Searches covered current source, ignored generated clients, and archived source rather than only the FullSpread directory. Nine oversized supplementary frontend matches were identified as compiled `.next-indexedex/dev/` vendor/application chunks, not additional authored bindings. Initial unbounded searches that timed out were not treated as negative evidence; bounded tree/source searches replaced them.

## Conclusions

The located inventory contains **637 preview rows across 626 files** and **744 exchangeIn rows across 702 files**. A row can group several cited call sites in one file; low-level decoders and interface declarations remain separate from that file's ordinary calls. Preview rows include 144 generated-client declarations and 82 retained historical rows. Exchange rows include 150 generated-client declarations and 123 retained historical rows. Counts describe this checkout, not deployed instances.

**Source observation:** the mandatory rate-provider path is a real one-word consumer. **Inference:** a successful 64-byte tuple would hit its current `data.length != 32` failure branch. The same inference applies to the wrapped provider and three reconciliation probes. The typed bindings expose only one amount and no refund field. This report does not claim that every old typed caller necessarily reverts on a longer response; no runtime experiment was performed. The projected `quoteAssets` ABI is separate and must not be silently conflated with the live preview branch.

In the tables, **Current decoding** and cited locations are source observations; **Disposition** and migration reasons are static-analysis conclusions. A path plus the adjacent Line column is the citation. All listed line numbers were read. Historical and generated entries are included for completeness, not as implementation instructions.

## Limits

- This is a live-checkout static inventory, not an atomic Git snapshot. No Git command, Forge command, test, install, deployment, wallet operation, or sub-agent was used.
- Build caches (Foundry out/cache, Hardhat artifact/build-info copies, compiled Next output, and vendored dependency binaries) are not counted as extra independent consumers. They describe or compile the source producers/consumers already identified. TypeChain client bindings were included separately because they are actual callable/decoding type bindings. No exhaustive bytecode-disassembly claim is made.
- Prose, historical JSON manifests, selector tables, call-data-only probes, event/error decoding, and unrelated one-word methods are not amount consumers of these two signatures. Their mere string or 32-byte matches were not promoted into migration rows.
- Runtime behavior, external consumers, deployments, and downstream repositories were not examined. A shared interface signature does not prove that every caller is connected to the new FullSpread family; the inventory deliberately includes all in-repo users of the old ABI rather than assuming such connectivity.
- Preserved audit sources and stale generated retired-family clients are recorded without changing or restoring them. No peer response was read and no council consensus is claimed.
- **This response does not authorize implementation or a PRD edit.** Only the assigned Astra response file was written.
